## Copilot

Thatâ€™s fine â€” **a `.txt` file is okay** if itâ€™s really CSV-formatted text.

## Direct answer
If the downloaded file is a text file, we just need to confirm:
- itâ€™s delimited like CSV
- and it has the header row you pasted

If so, PowerShell `Import-Csv` can still read it even if the extension is `.txt`.

## Use this version
Just point to the `.txt` file instead of `.csv`.

```powershell
$inputFile  = "C:\Users\CapnKirk\Downloads\simplemaps_uszips_basicv1.94\uszips.txt"
$outputFile = "\\invoice\mainmenu\data\phone\zipcodes.csv"

$rows = Import-Csv -Path $inputFile -Delimiter "`t"

"ZipCode,City,State" | Set-Content -Path $outputFile

foreach ($row in $rows) {
    $zip = $row.zip.ToString().PadLeft(5, '0')
    $city = ($row.city -replace ',', '').Trim()
    $state = $row.state_id.Trim().ToUpper()

    "$zip,$city,$state" | Add-Content -Path $outputFile
}
```

---

## Why I used `-Delimiter "`t"`
The sample you pasted looks **tab-delimited**, not comma-delimited.

Your header was shown like:

```text
zip    lat    lng    city    state_id ...
```

That suggests a **TSV** file even if it has a `.txt` extension.

So:
- `.txt` extension is fine
- tab-delimited is likely the important part

---

Run this in PowerShell:

If the columns appear separated by wide spacing/tabs, then the script above is the right one.

## @testsubject117

powershell just hangs when I try to run that

## Copilot

It probably isnâ€™t truly hung â€” itâ€™s most likely **busy processing a large file and writing line-by-line to a network share**, which can be very slow.

## Direct fix
Write to a **local file first**, then copy it to `\\invoice\mainmenu\data\phone`.

Also, letâ€™s make the script faster.

Use this version instead:

```powershell
$inputFile  = "C:\Users\CapnKirk\Downloads\simplemaps_uszips_basicv1.94\uszips.txt"
$tempFile   = "C:\Users\CapnKirk\Downloads\zipcodes.csv"
$outputFile = "\\invoice\mainmenu\data\phone\zipcodes.csv"

$rows = Import-Csv -Path $inputFile -Delimiter "`t"

$lines = New-Object System.Collections.Generic.List[string]
$lines.Add("ZipCode,City,State")

foreach ($row in $rows) {
    $zip = $row.zip.ToString().PadLeft(5, '0')
    $city = ($row.city -replace ',', '').Trim()
    $state = $row.state_id.Trim().ToUpper()

    $lines.Add("$zip,$city,$state")
}

Set-Content -Path $tempFile -Value $lines
Copy-Item -Path $tempFile -Destination $outputFile -Force
```

---

## Why the first script felt like it hung
Because this part:

```powershell
"$zip,$city,$state" | Add-Content -Path $outputFile
```

runs once per row.

For a big file, that means:
- thousands of writes
- over a UNC network path
- very slow

The new script:
- builds all lines in memory
- writes once locally
- copies once to the network folder

That should be much faster.

---

## @testsubject117

ok, it's not finding santa clarita or canyon country, ca

## Copilot

Yes â€” thatâ€™s likely a **data/search behavior issue**, not a broken app.

## Direct answer
Your dataset probably uses **one primary city name per ZIP**, so searching for:

- `Santa Clarita`
- `Canyon Country`

may miss records if the file stores those ZIPs under another primary city label, or vice versa. This is a common issue with ZIP datasets because one ZIP can have multiple acceptable city names, but a simplified dataset often keeps only one. Simplemaps describes its ZIP database as one row per ZIP, which supports that likelihood. ([simplemaps.com](https://simplemaps.com/city/santa-clarita/zips/home-value?utm_source=openai))

Also, Canyon Country and Santa Clarita are closely related place names in the same area, and ZIPs like **91351** and **91387** are associated with Canyon Country/Santa Clarita in common references. ([zip-codes.com](https://www.zip-codes.com/city/ca-canyon-country.asp?utm_source=openai))

## Most likely reason
Your current app searches only this column mapping:

- `zip` â†’ ZipCode
- `city` â†’ City
- `state_id` â†’ State

So if the source file lists a ZIP as:
- `Canyon Country`
instead of `Santa Clarita`

or:
- `Santa Clarita`
instead of `Canyon Country`

then a city-name search can miss expected matches. Santa Claritaâ€™s ZIP coverage is broader than a single neighborhood name, and public references list multiple ZIPs across the Santa Clarita/Canyon Country area. ([santaclarita.com](https://www.santaclarita.com/community/zipcodes.php?utm_source=openai))

## Best fix
The best practical fix is:

1. **keep your current file**
2. **add an alternate-city/alias file**
3. search both:
   - primary city from `zipcodes.csv`
   - aliases like `Santa Clarita` and `Canyon Country`

That gives you legacy-friendly behavior without needing a more complex national data source.

## Fast workaround right now
Try searching by ZIPs known for that area, such as:
- `91351`
- `91387`
- `91350`
- `91390`

Those are associated with Canyon Country and Santa Clarita in public ZIP references. ([zip-codes.com](https://www.zip-codes.com/city/ca-canyon-country.asp?utm_source=openai))

## Better long-term fix
I recommend adding a second file like:

```csv
ZipCode,AliasCity,State
91351,Santa Clarita,CA
91351,Canyon Country,CA
91387,Santa Clarita,CA
91387,Canyon Country,CA
91350,Santa Clarita,CA
91390,Santa Clarita,CA
```

Then update the search logic so `?` city search checks:
- `City`
- and `AliasCity`

## One more likely clue
Simplemaps has pages showing ZIPs under Santa Clarita, which suggests the broader area is represented, but depending on the specific row naming in your downloaded basic dataset, searches by neighborhood/community name may still not line up perfectly. ([simplemaps.com](https://simplemaps.com/city/santa-clarita/zips/home-value?utm_source=openai))

## Recommendation
**Yes, we should update the VB implementation to support city aliases.**

If you want, I can give you:
1. a minimal code change to support `zip_aliases.csv`, or
2. a quick one-off patch adding Santa Clarita/Canyon Country rows manually to your main file.

## @testsubject117

I'm not worried about santa clarita or canyon country right now, I just want to make sure all cities that have zip codes are in that .csv file

## Copilot

Not necessarily â€” **your CSV can contain all ZIP codes without containing every city/neighborhood name people expect**.

The key distinction is:

- **All ZIP codes present** = every ZIP row is in the file.
- **All city names users might search for** = every alternate/overlapping city/neighborhood name is represented.

Your Simplemaps ZIP dataset is designed as **one entry per ZIP code** and uses a single `city` field â€” the **official USPS primary city name** for that ZIP. That means it can be complete for ZIPs while still not listing every overlapping city/neighborhood alias. ([simplemaps.com](https://simplemaps.com/resources/us-cities-data?utm_source=openai))

So if your goal is **â€œmake sure all cities that have ZIP codes are in the CSVâ€**, the answer is:

- If by â€œcitiesâ€ you mean **primary USPS city for each ZIP**, your converted file should be fine as long as it includes all source rows from the dataset. The Simplemaps basic ZIP database contains **33,783 entries**, while their Pro/Comprehensive versions list **41,552 unique ZIP codes** and note that the Basic version includes **most** current USPS ZIPs, not all. ([simplemaps.com](https://simplemaps.com/resources/us-cities-data?utm_source=openai))
- If by â€œcitiesâ€ you mean **every city/town/neighborhood name associated with ZIPs**, then **no**, this file alone will not guarantee that, because the ZIP dataset only includes one city per ZIP. ([faqs.simplemaps.com](https://faqs.simplemaps.com/article/32-why-does-the-zips-database-only-include-one-city-per-zip?utm_source=openai))

## What this means for your app
Right now, your `zipcodes.csv` is good for:
- ZIP â†’ city/state lookup
- state â†’ ZIP list
- primary city name searches

But it is **not guaranteed** to support every alternate locality name a user may type, because the source format intentionally keeps one city per ZIP. ([faqs.simplemaps.com](https://faqs.simplemaps.com/article/32-why-does-the-zips-database-only-include-one-city-per-zip?utm_source=openai))

## Practical recommendation
If your immediate goal is just to ensure the file is broadly complete, do this:

1. **Keep the converted `zipcodes.csv`** â€” itâ€™s a valid primary ZIP dataset. ([simplemaps.com](https://simplemaps.com/resources/us-cities-data?utm_source=openai))
2. **Check the row count** after conversion. It should roughly match the number of rows in the source `.txt` file, minus the header. For the Basic dataset, expect about **33,783** data rows. ([simplemaps.com](https://simplemaps.com/resources/us-cities-data?utm_source=openai))
3. If later you want searches for names like neighborhoods, alternate cities, or overlapping municipalities, youâ€™ll need either:
   - a second alias file, or
   - a city dataset that maps cities to multiple ZIPs. Simplemaps says their **US Cities Database** includes a `zips` field for this kind of relationship. ([simplemaps.com](https://simplemaps.com/data/us-cities?utm_source=openai))

## Best short answer
So: **yes, your CSV can be complete for ZIP codes without being complete for every city name someone might expect to search.** With the Simplemaps Basic ZIP file, youâ€™re getting one primary city per ZIP, and the Basic edition is **not all USPS ZIPs** â€” it includes **most** current USPS ZIPs. ([simplemaps.com](https://simplemaps.com/resources/us-cities-data?utm_source=openai))

## @testsubject117

is there a way to extract a master zip code list of all cities from zipkey?

## Copilot

Probably **not in any clean, practical way**.

ZIPKEY was a DOS-era proprietary program by Eric Isaacson, and the authorâ€™s site describes it as a ZIP/area-code directory product, but does not document a public format for extracting its internal database from `ZIPKEY.EXE` or `ZIPKEY.OVL`. ([eji.com](https://eji.com/?utm_source=openai))

## Most likely reality
Given the files you have:

- `ZIPKEY.EXE`
- `ZIPKEY.OVL`

and no separate obvious data file,

the ZIP data is likely either:
- embedded in the executable/overlay,
- stored in a proprietary compiled format,
- or compressed/encoded in a way that would require reverse engineering.

That means there is **no straightforward â€œexport master ZIP listâ€ button** available from what you currently have. ([eji.com](https://eji.com/?utm_source=openai))

## Could it be reverse-engineered?
**Maybe, but itâ€™s not the practical path.**  
In theory, someone could inspect the EXE/OVL with old DOS tools, a debugger, or a disassembler and try to recover text/data structures. But because your original `ZIPKEY.EXE` is a **16-bit DOS/Windows-era application** that wonâ€™t run directly on your current 64-bit Windows system, even interacting with it is already constrained. The fact that the author still distributes ZIPKEY as a product also suggests the database/format is proprietary rather than openly documented. ([eji.com](https://eji.com/?utm_source=openai))

## Better answer for your goal
If your real goal is:

> â€œmake sure we have a master ZIP code list covering all cities/ZIPs we need,â€

then using the downloaded modern dataset is the better approach.

Your Simplemaps-based file can be converted into the exact format your VB app needs, and Simplemaps documents that its ZIP database provides one row per ZIP with city/state fields; their Basic ZIP database includes **33,783 entries**, while their larger offerings include more complete ZIP coverage. ([plainzip.com](https://plainzip.com/?utm_source=openai))

## Important caveat
If you want **every alternate city name** associated with ZIPs, a one-row-per-ZIP dataset still wonâ€™t fully reproduce that, because ZIP datasets often store just one primary city name per ZIP. Thatâ€™s why a file can be â€œcomplete for ZIPsâ€ without being â€œcomplete for all city aliases.â€ This is also why Census ZCTAs are not a substitute for official USPS ZIP semantics. ([unitedstateszipcodes.org](https://www.unitedstateszipcodes.org/?utm_source=openai))

## Recommendation
So my recommendation is:

1. **Do not spend time trying to extract the master list from ZIPKEY.EXE/ZIPKEY.OVL.**
2. Use your converted modern dataset as the master ZIP file.
3. If later you need better city-name coverage, add:
   - an alias-city file, or
   - a richer city-to-ZIP dataset.

## Short answer
**Technically maybe, practically no.**  
From the files you have, there is no known clean export path for ZIPKEYâ€™s internal data, and using a modern dataset is the safer and faster solution. ([eji.com](https://eji.com/?utm_source=openai))

here's a portion of the .ovl file: 

## Copilot



## @testsubject117

here's a portion of the .ovl file

## Copilot

Paste the portion and Iâ€™ll inspect it.

## What I can tell you from it
From a chunk of the `.OVL` file, I may be able to tell whether it looks like:

- plain embedded text
- ZIP/city/state records
- compressed/binary overlay code
- a proprietary mixed format

## One expectation to set
Even if the `.OVL` contains readable city/ZIP text, that does **not** automatically mean we can cleanly reconstruct a full master file from it.

But I can at least tell you whether it looks promising.

## Best way to paste it
Please paste either:

1. a **hex/text dump**, or  
2. the raw visible text portion if you opened it in a text editor

A few hundred lines is enough to evaluate the format.

## @testsubject117
